### Identity (reviewed)

Native Z80 implementation of the pyramid-hopping arcade concept for Amstrad Plus, with ASIC sprites, synthesized sound and separate cassette, disk and CPR cartridge packaging. Substantial native game implementation rather than emulation.
- https://github.com/chrisperver/qbert/blob/ccdc49db82b9038cfbdc03c05b85c77761534782/qbert21.asm
- https://github.com/chrisperver/qbert/blob/ccdc49db82b9038cfbdc03c05b85c77761534782/compile/makecart.bash
- https://github.com/chrisperver/qbert/blob/ccdc49db82b9038cfbdc03c05b85c77761534782/README.md
- https://github.com/chrisperver/qbert/commit/ccdc49db82b9038cfbdc03c05b85c77761534782
- https://github.com/chrisperver/qbert/tree/ccdc49db82b9038cfbdc03c05b85c77761534782

### Classification (reviewed)

qbert21.asm defines native pyramid/ball/Coily interaction, palette and ASIC sprite calls; compile scripts build actual native banks. No repository-wide licence file or grant found at pinned tree; published source availability does not imply permission to redistribute copyrighted Q*bert assets.

### Classification (reviewed)

Explicit bank maps and raw-sector sprite libraries; extensive emulator tests included. Source comments document disk-controller timing disagreement between cpcemu and Retro Virtual Machine, so emulator-specific assertions remain unverified. No project-wide licence grant found at pinned tree; audio/art provenance may impose separate constraints.

### Identity (reviewed)

Original Pipe Mania-inspired electronic-circuit puzzle for CPC 6128, written in ugBASIC and cross-compiled to native Z80. Grid routing, piece placement, distance and win checking are implemented in the included BASIC source.

### Identity (reviewed)

Original two/four-player BBC Model B territory-control game whose ownership state is encoded in 35 CMYK dither patterns. Native 6502 game has tanks, paint splats, ink reservoirs, AI opponents, keyboard remapping and 32 level layouts.
- https://github.com/rob-smallshire/dont-dither/blob/dd62dd2f3cbf3d5a6e7df909f41707df1bacf358/asm/game.asm

### Identity (reviewed)

Substantial 6502 conversion of the author’s ecological BBC game, originally released in mixed BASIC/assembler in 2022. The new engine owns per-frame diver/fish/shark/jellyfish collision, oxygen, sound and score while BASIC retains setup, scenery and level flow.
- https://github.com/sassquad/polymer-picker-6502/blob/1bbd808d963056a817e4ae2654b2707bd5c6b3d0/src/engine.asm

### Identity (reviewed)

Original author’s preservation and direct JavaScript port of FLY, published in The Micro User in July 1985. Includes detokenized/tokenized cassette source, a faithful classic browser presentation and an extended 3D game sharing line-number-annotated rules.

Source comments map rules to original BASIC lines and document discrepancies among cassette and disk editions. Actual rule engine is ported; browser rendering does not wrap a BBC CPU emulator. ### Identity (reviewed)

Work-in-progress native C64 adaptation of Kevin Edwards’s released BBC Galaforce source. A substantial C64 implementation replaces hardware setup, bitmap rendering, keyboard input and sound while retaining real alien types, waves and flight-pattern data from the original.
- https://github.com/radrestoration/Galaforce1C64/blob/f5db6cd19e39609ab331b21ee190ad3dfbce2e6b/src-c64/INIT.asm

### Identity (reviewed)

Native TRS-80 Model 4/4P recreation of Fallout’s terminal-hacking password puzzle, implemented in LS-DOS/TRSDOS BASIC with selectable difficulty and intelligence options. Complete readable listing plus interpreted, compiled and bootable disk forms are included.
- https://github.com/emersonrp/fallout.bas/blob/a8019ec19908ed8ee570d112f086d2b5e25b5b28/fallout.txt
- https://github.com/emersonrp/fallout.bas/blob/a8019ec19908ed8ee570d112f086d2b5e25b5b28/README.md

- https://github.com/emersonrp/fallout.bas/tree/a8019ec19908ed8ee570d112f086d2b5e25b5b28

### Classification (reviewed)

fallout.txt implements startup/options and password-game logic; repository includes CMD/BAS/DSK variants. Author warns compiler can produce crashes and cursor differs in compiled mode. No licence file or explicit redistribution grant found at pinned tree; Fallout branding is inspiration, not a demonstrated licence.
- https://github.com/emersonrp/fallout.bas/blob/a8019ec19908ed8ee570d112f086d2b5e25b5b28/fallout.txt

### Identity (reviewed)

Raymond Hill’s author-published 1992 Atari ST arcade game source, with single-player asteroid-style combat and a modem-linked two-player mode. The repository preserves original C plus extensive 68000 drawing, collision, input and modem routines, project definitions and a distributed PRG.
- https://github.com/gorhill/rayoid/blob/f3d0727eb93b66bd8526f5975a3b509ed70b2ec8/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Jeff and Steve Fulton’s 1990 Atari ST poker-dice game archive includes the original tokenized STOS BASIC GAME.BAS, compiled PRG, graphics, sound/data and detailed original instructions. Read-only structural analysis identifies the Lionpoulos STOS header and a complete ordered tokenized BASIC program, not merely a binary with incidental strings.
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/blob/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4/README.md
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/blob/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4/GAME.BAS
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/blob/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4/ZAMDOC.DOC
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/commit/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4

### Classification (reviewed)

GAME.BAS is 149,220 bytes. It begins with the Lionpoulos STOS signature. Starting at byte offset 78, 583 consecutive big-endian record-length/line-number records validate without gaps or overruns, with strictly increasing line numbers from 1 to 21020. Records end at offset 40162 with a zero-length terminator; 40,084 record bytes plus the 2-byte terminator exactly equal the header’s 40,086-byte program-length field. Tokenized commands, typed numeric/string/variable payloads and game-specific player/dice/category/scoring strings are visible. This establishes recoverable structured source rather than only incidental strings. Full detokenization/build was not performed; remaining file bytes contain attached banks/data. No explicit software license located. Archive also includes a TOS ROM image whose redistribution rights are not established; promotion concerns the source project and does not authorize redistributing that ROM.
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/blob/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4/README.md
- https://github.com/8bitjeff/atarist_zamboozal_game/blob/0b071ecb6373a2d2464e8afc1ef3f0de1daa33f4/GAME.BAS

### Identity (reviewed)

Author archive of the historical GEM-PC adaptation of an oil-company economic strategy game originally written for Atari ST GEM in the 1980s. The substantial C source tree preserves economics, drilling, transportation, newspaper events, GEM UI and IBM DOS compatibility routines; comments include 1986 development dates. This record covers the recovered PC implementation, not an established native Atari ST build.
- https://github.com/afotherg/The-Oil-Game/blob/1f30c58e0cad4cfe1e2cd3eaedcac33ccecf04d2/src/MAIN.C

### Classification (reviewed)

Broad substantial historical implementation and resource files, including MAIN.C, ECON.C, BUILD.C, MONTH.C, transportation, accounts and GEM UI. HEADER.H unconditionally includes ibm.h; IBM.C includes dos.h and uses intdos; IBM.H uses halloc. These identify the checked-in runtime adaptation as IBM PC, not Atari ST. PCjs page is a wrapper only, not the basis for promotion. Earlier pass15 deferral mixed architecture qualification, lack of ST build recipe and unspecified licensing; none negate the substantial recovered GEM-PC source when accurately scoped. No explicit license located; historical source availability is not an open-source license grant.

### Identity (reviewed)

Author-released 1990–1993 Amiga tools: Hunk executable/object inspector, Hunkhack self-decompressing-file manipulator, UnStuff Macintosh SIT extractor, DXF-to-SDF Sega CD model converter and line-ending converter. C implementations preserve real parsing and conversion logic.
- https://github.com/pheinrich/amigatools/blob/d49295d442d333a6f641b3e667aed27054887701/README

### Identity (reviewed)

Per Olofsson’s joystick-operated native Amiga game menu, with OCS/AGA layouts, directory navigation, responsive asynchronous IFF screenshots, slideshows, per-game scripts and configuration. Full Amiga E sources are provided for launcher, menu and helper.

### Identity (reviewed)

Grzegorz Kraszewski’s system-friendly native Amiga implementation of the line-untangling graph puzzle, containing window/menu interaction through Amiga Intuition, coordinate transforms, graph generation/solving tests and level loading.

### Identity (reviewed)

Robin Southern’s native AmigaOS 3.x C game framework supplies event, time, memory, window, input and planar/chunky framebuffer implementations, including a 68000-family chunky-to-planar assembly component. This is a reusable native framework, not an emulator wrapper.
- https://github.com/betajaen/synthwave/blob/96b5c6a07ec36615055dd0e05bb71b8359852c3b/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Keli Hlodversson’s Conway’s Game of Life implementation performs cellular-automaton work using the Atari ST BLiTTER, wrapped in a GEM application. Source includes BLiTTER register definitions, counting tables, update routines and window/display code.
- https://github.com/kelihlodversson/blitlife/blob/a6b66471f14dde70434561ba297416fb94f9ec78/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Python/Pygame reimplementation of the Atari ST edition of Super Sprint, aiming to reproduce original gameplay while adding custom cars/tracks/music and improving waits and controls. The source implements vehicle dynamics, collisions, track logic, AI opponents, menus and asset managers.

### Identity (reviewed)

Sandor Drieënhuizen’s reusable Atari ST 68000 assembly subsystem exposes keyboard scan/ASCII tables, last scan code and two joystick states to GFA BASIC via an inline binary and documented fixed jump table. Full assembly and GFA demo are included.
- https://github.com/sandord/ikbd4gfa/blob/83a12d39db9218bd67f1b497505ff23f519aa0ce/README.md
- https://github.com/sandord/ikbd4gfa/blob/83a12d39db9218bd67f1b497505ff23f519aa0ce/src/IKBD4GFA.S

- https://github.com/sandord/ikbd4gfa/commit/83a12d39db9218bd67f1b497505ff23f519aa0ce

### Classification (reviewed)

Useful bounded native subsystem with full interrupt/device code and working-call examples; not an undocumented binary blob. CC0-1.0 Universal, explicitly identified in source and LICENSE.

### Identity (reviewed)

Cross-platform installation/build integration for the EPSON/TASKING S1C88 compiler, including Pokémon Mini headers, locator configuration, startup assembly and an executable hello-world source example. This is an SDK integration project, not a reconstruction of the proprietary compiler.

### Identity (reviewed)

Long-running Delphi/Lazarus emulator suite with native Pascal CPU and device implementations and reusable arcade, computer and console drivers, including relatively uncommon Casio PV-1000 and Super Cassette Vision.

### Identity (reviewed)

Independent TypeScript DAP frontend for cc65 Lynx programs, driving Gearlynx through TCP debug-monitor and framebuffer protocols; source/symbol parsing, breakpoints, stepping, overlay selection and memory visualization.
- https://github.com/BrianPeek/gearlynx-vscode/blob/f121cb4af469dda39d14d82f489b045551509fed/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Preservation of the 1994–1997 multiplayer, isometric, turn-based DOS tactics game by Niko Nevatie and Kari Luojus, including the game, editors and original Turbo Pascal/i386 assembler source.
- https://github.com/nnevatie/paybacktime2/blob/c46bc3aa03ea956c04971d3b7bc193bec67b7d51/src/3DGAME.PAS

### Identity (reviewed)

Original lightweight DOS game launcher created in 2022, with metadata filtering, play-time tracking and a PathMinder-inspired text UI. Pascal implementation launches games using a separately credited ExecWithSwap module.

Original two-player MS-DOS action game by Gered King for the June 2021 GDR 4x4x4 challenge, with a Pascal state machine, match simulation, map editor, assets and GDlib mode-13h routines.

### Identity (reviewed)

Ongoing Pascal version of Andrew Danson’s DOS platform game, started around 1999 and retaining levels from his earlier QBasic version. Includes engine, XT and standard builds, level editor, graphics modes and sound drivers.

### Identity (reviewed)

Original low-level keyboard scan-code diagnostic for DOS, with IRQ1 handling and an alternative standalone boot-sector mode; build tooling can package it into FreeDOS floppy/CD images.
- https://github.com/freddy77/doskeys/blob/1fdd597cb03af070d65b1e5e196ab44b0af90916/kbd.asm
- https://github.com/freddy77/doskeys/blob/1fdd597cb03af070d65b1e5e196ab44b0af90916/Makefile

- https://github.com/freddy77/doskeys/commit/1fdd597cb03af070d65b1e5e196ab44b0af90916

Source uses PUSHA/POPA, so CPU is x86 with 80186+ instructions despite 16-bit output; Linux is a documented build host, not a source CPU. No explicit license grant found; bundled FreeDOS image has its own distribution obligations.

### Identity (reviewed)

Source copied from an operating MR12.7 Multics instance and reconstructed from simulated backup tapes with mxload; preserves and extends the Bull HN/MIT source release, including substantial PL/I and ALM system implementations.
- https://github.com/dancrossnyc/multics/blob/dc291689edf955c660e57236da694630e2217151/README.md
- https://github.com/dancrossnyc/multics/blob/dc291689edf955c660e57236da694630e2217151/COPYRIGHT.md

- https://github.com/dancrossnyc/multics/commit/dc291689edf955c660e57236da694630e2217151

### Classification (reviewed)

Objects and most .bind files are excluded, and README warns that some source files could have been accidentally skipped. A substantial source archive, not the separately tracked DPS8M simulator. COPYRIGHT.md documents Bull HN/MIT permissive historical release but expressly notes missing redistribution notices for subsequent revival changes; do not present the entire MR12.7 archive as unambiguously MIT licensed.

### Identity (reviewed)

Collection of BCause, a single-pass B-to-x86-64 compiler, and bbesm, a B-written compiler for BESM-6 under Dubna; also preserves the PDP-7 B compiler that bbesm explicitly derives from.

PDP-7 b.c is GPL3-marked. Mixed/inconsistent licensing must be retained as a caveat.

### Classification (reviewed)

README claims BESM-6 and Elbrous-B revival; inspected tree has Elbrous-B source but no BESM-6 backend. Catalogue this as historical Elbrous-B source, not a proven working BESM-6 compiler. Modern PCC core files contain permissive Caldera/BSD-style notices. Historical Elbrous-B files examined have author/date headers without a complete permission grant; coverage of those files is unresolved.

### Identity (reviewed)

Hardware-specific port of Dan Banay’s Smalltalk-80 VM to Circle, replacing desktop SDL/OS facilities with Raspberry Pi kernel, framebuffer, USB input and SD/FAT storage integration.

### Identity (reviewed)

Port of the historical Oberon System to an Oberon-to-LuaJIT compiler/runtime and Qt backend, preserving substantial text/viewer/editor/application modules and replacing original inner system services.
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/OberonSystem/blob/11da109e64fd55a73bd40cc69ed5d5cbc9a57c38/README.md
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/OberonSystem/blob/11da109e64fd55a73bd40cc69ed5d5cbc9a57c38/Texts.Mod

- https://github.com/rochus-keller/OberonSystem/commit/11da109e64fd55a73bd40cc69ed5d5cbc9a57c38

### Classification (reviewed)

Explicit prototype; README says Texts.WriteReal and System.Copy have known issues. Not a standalone full-machine emulator. QtDisplay.cpp explicitly offers GPLv2 or GPLv3 for that backend. Complete original Oberon module licence coverage is not established in this repository; no single root license file found.

Full Bell Labs/Vita Nuova Inferno system source including native and hosted kernels, Dis VM, Limbo compiler, libraries, device services and applications, maintained as a portable distributed operating environment.

### Classification (reviewed)

Complete native original software continuation, not a reconstruction of Plan9. Target support varies; INSTALL labels some included old ports unverified. NOTICE says bulk MIT, component-specific notices apply; FreeType terms and Bigelow/Holmes font redistribution restriction require preservation.

INSTALL requires editing mkconfig ROOT/SYSHOST/OBJTYPE, bootstrapping mk with makemk.sh, then mk install; native and hosted trees have mkfiles. No candidate software, build script, compiler, interpreter, emulator or tests were executed. Build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown. ### Relationships (reviewed)

Originally Bell Labs, maintained by Vita Nuova. Uses 9P/Plan9 concepts and shares some source, but is a distinct operating system with Limbo/Dis.
